> Previously, we covered what MetaMask Snaps are, how they work, and some of the vulnerability research OtterSec has done. This is the final thread in the series covering our demonstration of the vulnerability we found and our conclusions.
>
> If you missed our last post, start here: https://t.co/qJdT3HI3sY

**Quoted post (full text):**

    #### Quoted post — @osec_io — 2023-11-20 20:11 UTC (2023-11-20 21:11 WAT)
    
    - URL: https://x.com/osec_io/status/1726694785409556552
    - Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 2, impression 14856, like 21, quote 1, reply 1, retweet 4
    
    > Now that we've covered some of the basics of Metamask Snaps, let's talk about some of the research OtterSec has done on vulnerabilities and possible attacks. This is thread 2 in our series.
    >
    > If you missed our introduction, start here: https://t.co/N68zcY14uI
    
    **Quoted post (full text):**
    
        #### Quoted post — @osec_io — 2023-11-02 11:51 UTC (2023-11-02 12:51 WAT)
        
        - URL: https://x.com/osec_io/status/1720045911571574785
        - Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 8, impression 12978, like 45, quote 2, reply 1, retweet 14

> While smart contract security is important, there are other simple attack vectors, like Discord, that have been used to socially engineer community members for their Discord credentials and private keys.
>
> Let's dive into the recent attacks on #Aptos Discords 👇 1/9 [pic.x.com/PS0QJsO35w]

**Media:**
- photo:  — no alt text provided by author


### Thread part 2 — @osec_io — 2023-01-12 08:51 UTC (2023-01-12 09:51 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/osec_io/status/1613458745991757824
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 0, impression 1414, like 11, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 1

> After a mod expresses interest in the job opportunity, they will be invited to a "shell" server, where a bot prompts them to enter their Discord credentials for verification.
>
> Entering their credentials will transfer their authority token to the "shell" server owner. 3/9 [pic.x.com/nr2xtACpr6]

**Media:**
- media `3_1613456398767079426` (details not returned by the API)


### Thread part 3 — @osec_io — 2023-01-12 08:51 UTC (2023-01-12 09:51 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/osec_io/status/1613458748638371843
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 0, impression 1314, like 10, quote 1, reply 2, retweet 0

> The "shell" server owner now has full read + write permissions to the accounts that the moderator once had access to. Thus, allowing full access to the servers + channels where trusted team members provide their community with trusted information. 4/9 [pic.x.com/TrRJK10goE]

**Media:**
- photo: https://pbs.twimg.com/media/FmQitr4XgAIHd2j.jpg — no alt text provided by author


### Thread part 4 — @osec_io — 2023-01-12 08:51 UTC (2023-01-12 09:51 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/osec_io/status/1613458751704686594
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 0, impression 888, like 7, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 0

> The malicious party then monitors exactly which permissions they have in order to execute a phishing attempt aimed at the broader community. 
>
> This method is frequently (and successfully) used to extract assets from user wallets within community Discords. 5/9


### Thread part 5 — @osec_io — 2023-01-12 08:51 UTC (2023-01-12 09:51 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/osec_io/status/1613458754288091137
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 0, impression 1005, like 8, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 0

> The teams involved have made the following remediations:
> - Remove all moderator perms
> - Review audit logs to track malicious behavior
> - Review role perms to limit admin capabilities
> - Notify server team members to reset their pw
> - Require 2FA for moderator actions
> 6/9


### Thread part 6 — @osec_io — 2023-01-12 08:51 UTC (2023-01-12 09:51 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/osec_io/status/1613458756972462080
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 0, impression 933, like 10, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 0

> General rule of thumb on general Discord security: 
>
> Do not use verification bots that send DMs to your users for identity verification. This often allows malicious actors to front run the verification message in order to extract valuable user data. 7/9


### Thread part 7 — @osec_io — 2023-01-12 08:51 UTC (2023-01-12 09:51 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/osec_io/status/1613458759422038017
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 0, impression 1333, like 7, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 0

> Additionally, leave your DMs off, and do your own DD when clicking links from "bots" or unfamiliar users that you may share a server with. 8/9


### Thread part 8 — @osec_io — 2023-01-12 08:52 UTC (2023-01-12 09:52 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/osec_io/status/1613458761783410691
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 0, impression 1951, like 16, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 4

>
> 👇 https://twitter.com/NotDeGhost/status/1567211967361736704

**Quoted post (full text):**

    #### Quoted post — @1038484278810570752 — 2022-09-06 18:03 UTC (2022-09-06 19:03 WAT)
    
    - URL: https://x.com/1038484278810570752/status/1567211967361736704
    - Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 24, impression 0, like 118, quote 3, reply 7, retweet 36
    
    > How do we make smart contracts structurally safer? 
    >

## 2022-04-18 · Thread · 1/ We were engaged by @port_finance to audit their fixed-rate lending program, Sundial. In our audit


Tags (automatic): Smart contract / DeFi


### Post — @osec_io — 2022-04-18 16:08 UTC (2022-04-18 17:08 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/osec_io/status/1516086365393350657
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 7, impression 0, like 33, quote 2, reply 2, retweet 2

> 1/ We were engaged by @port_finance to audit their fixed-rate lending program, Sundial. In our audit, we found 3 bugs that could lead to loss of funds for Port and its users.
>
> Let’s take a deep dive into OS-PRT-ADV-00, a surprisingly subtle rounding bug in the liquidation handler [pic.x.com/gb5vb3Z8KE]

**Media:**
- photo:  — no alt text provided by author


### Thread part 2 — @osec_io — 2022-04-18 16:08 UTC (2022-04-18 17:08 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/osec_io/status/1516086372938878979
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 0, impression 0, like 2, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 0

> 2/ As part of Sundial’s liquidation design, the reward liquidators receive from liquidations is rounded up to the nearest whole unit. This is intended to “round against” the user being liquidated. [pic.x.com/pDFCKTtbqu]

**Media:**
- photo: https://pbs.twimg.com/media/FQozwTMVcAEJEBY.jpg — no alt text provided by author


### Thread part 3 — @osec_io — 2022-04-18 16:08 UTC (2022-04-18 17:08 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/osec_io/status/1516086375673520131
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 0, impression 0, like 1, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 0

> 3/ For example, if the account had BTC collateral and USDC obligations, attempting to liquidate 1 USDC token should give back 0.00002 BTC. This would get rounded up to 1 BTC, meaning the liquidator receives around 40,000x more than they should!


### Thread part 4 — @osec_io — 2022-04-18 16:08 UTC (2022-04-18 17:08 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/osec_io/status/1516086378022330371
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 0, impression 0, like 1, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 0

> 4/ Is this a security issue? At its core, this design allows a liquidator to extract far more collateral per liquidation than they otherwise should. As with many liquidation bugs we’ve reported, the first step is to demonstrate that we can decrease the health of the account.


### Thread part 5 — @osec_io — 2022-04-18 16:08 UTC (2022-04-18 17:08 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/osec_io/status/1516086380199231488
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 0, impression 0, like 2, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 1

> 5/ If a liquidation can decrease the health of an account undergoing liquidations, we could repeatedly liquidate until the account eventually becomes undercollateralized! This would result in a loss of funds for the protocol.


### Thread part 6 — @osec_io — 2022-04-18 16:08 UTC (2022-04-18 17:08 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/osec_io/status/1516086387002404867
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 0, impression 0, like 3, quote 0, reply 2, retweet 0

> 6/ To execute this proof-of-concept, we needed to first create an account collateralized with a high-value token, while taking out a low-value loan. We then repeatedly self liquidate a small quantity of the low-value token, receiving one of the high-value token back. [pic.x.com/1yAUkXbPmA]

**Media:**
- photo: https://pbs.twimg.com/media/FQoz9osVIAIinm7.jpg — no alt text provided by author


### Thread part 7 — @osec_io — 2022-04-18 16:08 UTC (2022-04-18 17:08 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/osec_io/status/1516086394082504704
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 0, impression 0, like 2, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 0

> 7/ In the end, we managed to drain all of our collateral, leaving behind a massively undercollateralized account. [pic.x.com/68AqoC08t6]

**Media:**
- photo: https://pbs.twimg.com/media/FQo0D3YVIAMZ88h.jpg — no alt text provided by author


### Thread part 8 — @osec_io — 2022-04-18 16:09 UTC (2022-04-18 17:09 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/osec_io/status/1516086401598574592
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 0, impression 0, like 1, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 0

> 8/ To fix this, we worked with Port Finance to ensure that liquidations almost always increase the health of the account. The only edge case to handle is with very small obligations, which might not be possible to liquidate without decreasing account health. [pic.x.com/zR4ynHgsKK]

**Media:**
- photo: https://pbs.twimg.com/media/FQo0JaMVIAIy81x.jpg — no alt text provided by author


### Thread part 9 — @osec_io — 2022-04-18 16:09 UTC (2022-04-18 17:09 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/osec_io/status/1516086405507678211
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 0, impression 0, like 3, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 0

> 9/ We also disclosed two other loss of funds issues in this report, related to interest calculations and improperly handled configuration changes. For more information, see the full audit report: 
>
> https://github.com/port-finance/sundial/blob/master/audits/port-finance-sundial-audit-public.pdf

**Links in post:** https://github.com/port-finance/sundial/blob/master/audits/port-finance-sundial-audit-public.pdf

## 2022-04-16 · Thread · 1/ In our recent audit report on @JetProtocol’s governance program, we listed 13 findings and 4 vuln


### Post — @osec_io — 2022-04-16 16:53 UTC (2022-04-16 17:53 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/osec_io/status/1515372872939675650
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 49, impression 0, like 156, quote 7, reply 3, retweet 26

> 1/ In our recent audit report on @JetProtocol’s governance program, we listed 13 findings and 4 vulnerabilities. One of these vulnerabilities stand out from the others: OS-JET-ADV-01
>
> Let’s take a deep dive into this rounding error, the implications, as well as its exploitability https://t.co/MYI6MARLVE

**Media:**
- photo:  — no alt text provided by author


### Thread part 2 — @osec_io — 2022-04-16 16:53 UTC (2022-04-16 17:53 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/osec_io/status/1515372875716341762
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 1, impression 0, like 7, quote 0, reply 2, retweet 0

> 2/ In Jet’s original conversion code between tokens and shares, all rounding was done via floor division. This meant that the correctness of the code depended on floor division to not be in favor of the user.


### Thread part 3 — @osec_io — 2022-04-16 16:53 UTC (2022-04-16 17:53 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/osec_io/status/1515372881655476226
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 0, impression 0, like 5, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 0

> 3/ Unfortunately, due to how conversions were done, this was not the case. Users who were supposed to pay 2.5 tokens only had to pay 2. In other words, they could pay up to one less token than they should. Could a pool of staked tokens be extracted through this rounding error? https://t.co/LFJnrtFFJu

**Media:**
- photo: https://pbs.twimg.com/media/FQetBvXVEAMooYB.jpg — no alt text provided by author


### Thread part 4 — @osec_io — 2022-04-16 16:53 UTC (2022-04-16 17:53 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/osec_io/status/1515372884775956480
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 0, impression 0, like 6, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 0

> 4/ In order to determine the severity of this vulnerability, we needed to make sure that this rounding bug was repeatedly exploitable. In other words, there are no “end” states where the token and share amounts collapse to.


### Thread part 5 — @osec_io — 2022-04-16 16:53 UTC (2022-04-16 17:53 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/osec_io/status/1515372886952980498
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 0, impression 0, like 6, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 0

> 5/ Before writing a full POC in rust, we laid out our assumptions. The basic operations a user can do to influence the state are: 
>
> 1. Adding stake (shares &amp; tokens) to the pool
> 2. Removing stake by unbonding and withdrawing 
> 3. Sending tokens directly to the vault stake account.

### Thread part 2 — @osec_io — 2022-04-14 15:53 UTC (2022-04-14 16:53 WAT)

- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 0, impression 0, like 3, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 0

> 2/ Conversion Rate Abuse:
>
> Jet’s original design involved a dynamic conversion rate between shares and tokens. However, unbonded shares were not counted in the conversion. An unprivileged user could manipulate the exchange rate by depositing &amp; unbonding large amounts of tokens! https://t.co/sTfEG82MvD

**Media:**
- photo: https://pbs.twimg.com/media/FQUM_NfVsAIj6Ea.jpg — no alt text provided by author

- URL: https://x.com/osec_io/status/1514632938427539459
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 0, impression 0, like 3, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 0

> 3/ To fix this, we worked with the Jet team to revamp the conversions into two separate pools. Unbonding stake transfers the shares directly, mitigating any conversion manipulation vulnerabilities! https://t.co/iSTRI8fgv5

**Media:**
- photo: https://pbs.twimg.com/media/FQUNRQyVkAI4qv8.png — no alt text provided by author


### Thread part 4 — @osec_io — 2022-04-14 15:53 UTC (2022-04-14 16:53 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/osec_io/status/1514632943527874562
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 1, impression 0, like 2, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 0

> 4/ Conversion Rounding error:
>
> When writing Solana smart contract programs, it’s critical to always round against the user. Jet’s original conversions used Rust’s default division, which always floored the conversions. https://t.co/9QpuaARGvq

**Media:**
- photo: https://pbs.twimg.com/media/FQUNZwcVsAA6qx7.png — no alt text provided by author

- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 0, impression 0, like 2, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 0

> 5/ To fix this, we worked with the Jet team to introduce rounding directions into the codebase. This way, the code will always properly round amounts against the user. https://t.co/E1LoonLQSM
